(see Section
5.1
).
Set

\ast_{c}=\otimes_{e}\,\ast_{e}\in H_{c}

where
\otimes_{e}
is the unordered tensor product over all unoriented edges
e
of
P
.

For a vertex
x
of
P
, consider the link graph
\Gamma_{x}\subset\partial B_{x}
where
B_{x}\subset M
is a
P
-
cone neighborhood of
x
(see Section
6.2
). Recall from Section
6.3
that the
\chi
-labeling of
P
turns
\Gamma_{x}
into a 1-spherical
\chi
-graph in
\partial B_{x}\cong S^{2}
. The coloring
c
of
P
makes it
\mathcal{C}
-colored: every edge of
\Gamma_{x}
lies in a region
r
of
P
and is colored with
c(r)\in I
.
In this way,
\Gamma_{x}
becomes a 1-spherical
\mathcal{C}
-colored
\chi
-graph in
\partial B_{x}
. It is denoted
\Gamma_{x}^{c}
.
Since
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(\mathbb{1})=\Bbbk
, Section
5.8
yields a vector

\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Gamma^{c}_{x})\in H(\Gamma_{x}^{c})^{\star}

where we denote by
U^{\star}=\mathrm{Hom}_{\Bbbk}(U,\Bbbk)
the dual of a
\Bbbk
-module
U
.
Note that the
\chi
-cyclic
\mathcal{C}
-set associated with any vertex
v
of
\Gamma_{x}^{c}
is canonically isomorphic to the
\chi
-cyclic
\mathcal{C}
-set
P_{e}
, where
e
is the edge of
P
containing
v
and oriented away from
x
. Therefore, there are canonical isomorphisms

H(\Gamma_{x}^{c})\simeq\otimes_{e_{x}}\,H_{c}(e_{x})\qquad\text{and}\qquad H(\Gamma_{x}^{c})^{\star}\simeq\otimes_{e_{x}}\,H_{c}(e_{x})^{\star},

where
e_{x}
runs over all edges of
P
incident to
x
and oriented away from
x
. (An edge with both endpoints in
x
appears in each of these tensor products twice with opposite orientations.) The tensor product of the latter isomorphisms over all vertices
x
of
P
yields a
\Bbbk
-linear isomorphism

\bigotimes_{x}H(\Gamma_{x}^{c})^{\star}\simeq\bigotimes_{x}\bigotimes_{e_{x}}H_{c}(e_{x})^{\star}\simeq\bigotimes_{e}H_{c}(e)^{\star}\simeq H_{c}^{\star}

where
e
runs over all oriented edges of
P
.
The image under this isomorphism of the unordered tensor product
\bigotimes_{x}\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Gamma^{c}_{x})
, where
x
runs over all
vertices of
P
, is a vector
V_{c}\in H_{c}^{\star}
.
We evaluate
V_{c}
on
\ast_{c}
and set

|c|=V_{c}(\ast_{c})\in\Bbbk.

Finally, set

(27)

|M,g|_{\mathcal{C}}=\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})^{-|M\setminus P|}\sum_{c}\,\,\dim(c)\,|c|\in\Bbbk,

where
|M\setminus P|
is the number of components of
M\setminus P
and
c
runs over all colorings of
P
. The right-hand side of (
27
) is well-defined because there are finitely many colorings of
P
.

Theorem 7.1
.

|M,g|_{\mathcal{C}}
is a topological invariant of the closed
\chi
-manifold
(M,g)
independent of the choice of
P
and
I
.

We prove Theorem
7.1
in Section
7.6
using moves on
\chi
-skeletons introduced in Section
6.8
. The invariant of Theorem
7.1
is nontrivial and may even distinguish homotopy classes of phantom maps (i.e., of maps inducing trivial homomorphisms on homotopy groups), see for instance Example
7.10
below.

Note that if
g
is the homotopy class of a constant map, then
|M,g|_{\mathcal{C}}=|M|_{\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}}
where the right hand side is the Turaev-Viro-Barrett-Westbury state sum invariant of the oriented closed 3-dimensional manifold
M
derived from the spherical fusion category
\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1}
(see
[
BW
,
TVi4
]
). Moreover Theorem
7.1
generalizes the construction of
[
TVi1
]
which produces an invariant
of closed oriented 3-dimensional manifolds endowed with a homotopy class of maps to a
K(G,1)
space, where
G
is a group, from any spherical
G
-fusion category (over
\Bbbk
) whose neutral component has invertible dimension in
\Bbbk
. Indeed, the classifying space
B\chi
is aspherical if and only if
\chi
is injective. And if
\chi\colon E\to H
is injective (for example
E=1
), then
B\chi
is a
K(G,1)
space with
G=\mathrm{Coker}(\chi)=H/{\mathrm{Im}}(\chi)
, the category
\mathcal{C}
is a
G
-fusion category (in the sense of
[
TVi1
]
) whose neutral component has invertible dimension
\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})
(see Remark
4.11
), and the invariant of closed
\chi
-manifolds from
[
TVi1
]
defined using this
G
-fusion category is equal to the invariant
|\cdot|_{\mathcal{C}}
of Theorem
7.1
.

We illustrate Theorem
7.1
with the following two examples.

7.2.
Example

Consider the closed 3-dimensional manifold
S^{1}\times S^{2}
with the product orientation. Pick a point
p\in S^{1}
and an embedded loop
\gamma\subset S^{2}
. The 2
-
polyhedron
P=(\{p\}\times S^{2})\cup(S^{1}\times\gamma)
has two disk regions and one annulus region. Stratified by the based loop
\{p\}\times\gamma
, the polyhedron
P
is a skeleton of
S^{1}\times S^{2}
with one vertex, one edge, and two
P
-balls.
Any
\chi
-labeling of
P
is fully determined by a tuple
\ell=(x,y,z,e)\in H^{3}\times E
such that
\chi(e)=z^{-1}xzy
.
Here
x,y
are the
H
-labels of the disk regions,
z
is the
H
-label of the annulus region, and
e
is the
E
-label of the edge of
P
with respect to the front
P
-ball branch and for the choices of orientations as in the next picture which represents a neighborhood of the vertex of
P
:

\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{skel-S1S2.eps}}}\;\;.

By Lemma
6.1
, this

\chi
-labeling determines a homotopy class
g_{\ell}\in[S^{1}\times S^{2},B\chi]
. Then

\displaystyle|S^{1}\times S^{2},g_{\ell}|_{\mathcal{C}}\overset{(i)}{=}\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})^{-2}\hskip-10.00002pt\sum_{i\in I_{x},\,j\in I_{y},\,k\in I_{z}}\hskip-10.00002pt\dim(i)\dim(j)N^{\mathbb{1},e}_{k^{*}\otimes i\otimes k\otimes j}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})^{-1}\sum_{k\in I_{z}}\dim(k)^{2}\overset{(iii)}{=}1_{\Bbbk}.

Here
(i)
follows from (
27
), (
19
), and (
12
),
(ii)
from Lemma
4.1
(with
g=x
,
h=y
,
X=k^{*}
,
Y=k
,
m=i
,
n=j
), and
(iii)
from (
8
).

7.3.
Example

Consider the lens space
L(p,q)
where
p,q
are coprime integers with
1\leq q<p
. Recall from Example
6.4
that any
g\in[L(p,q),B\chi]
is of the form
g=g_{h,e}
for some
(h,e)\in H\times E
satisfying
\chi(e)=h^{p}
and
{\vphantom{{e}}}{}^{h}{e}=e
. By applying Formula (
27
) to the
\chi
-skeleton
P
of
(L(p,q),g)
induced by
(h,e)
as in Example
6.4
, we obtain

|L(p,q),g|_{\mathcal{C}}=\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})^{-1}\sum_{i\in I_{h}}\,\,\dim(i)\,\mathrm{Tr}\bigl((\sigma_{i})^{q}\bigr)\in\Bbbk,

where
\sigma_{i}
is the
\Bbbk
-linear endomorphism of
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}^{e}(\mathbb{1},i^{\otimes p})
defined by

\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag u\mapsto\sigma_{i}(u)\;=\;\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{sigma-lens.eps}}}

and
\mathrm{Tr}
is the standard trace of
\Bbbk
-linear endomorphisms. This follows from the description of the
\chi
-graph
\Gamma
associated with the single vertex of
P
given in Example
6.4
, the definition of the invariant
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Gamma)
, and Formula (
19
) which provides a computation of the contraction vector associated with the single edge of
P
.

7.4.
A conjecture

Consider the spherical
\chi
-fusion category
\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}
of Example
4.12
. It follows directly from the definitions that for any closed
\chi
-manifold
(M,g)
,

(28)

|M,g|_{\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}}=1_{\Bbbk}.

More generally, consider the spherical
\chi
-fusion category
\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}^{\omega}
obtained by twisting the
\chi
-category
\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}
with a normalized 3-cocycle
\omega
for
\chi
with values in
\Bbbk^{*}
(see Section
4.16
). Let
[\omega]\in H^{3}(B\chi,\Bbbk^{*})
be the cohomology class induced by
\omega
. We conjecture that for any closed
\chi
-manifold
(M,g)
,

(29)

|M,g|_{\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}^{\omega}}=\langle g^{*}([\omega]),[M]\rangle,

where
g^{*}([\omega])\in H^{3}(M,\Bbbk^{*})
is the pullback of
[\omega]
along
g
,
[M]\in H_{3}(M,\mathbb{Z})
is the fundamental class of
M
, and
\langle\,,\,\rangle\colon H^{3}(M,\Bbbk^{*})\times H_{3}(M,\mathbb{Z})\to\Bbbk
is the Kronecker pairing. Note that the computations in
[
TVi4
, Appendix H]
imply that this conjecture is true when
E=1
(and so
\omega
is a 3-cocycle for
H
), that is, for the crossed modules of the form
1\to H
with
H
a group.

7.5.
Invariants of 3-manifolds

Let
M
be a closed oriented 3-dimensional manifold. It follows from Theorem
7.1
that if
\mathfrak{a}=(\mathfrak{a}_{g})_{g\in[M,B\chi]}
is a family of elements of
\Bbbk
such that
\mathfrak{a}_{g}=0
for all but finitely many
g
, then the linear combination

I_{\mathcal{C},\mathfrak{a}}(M)=\!\!\sum_{g\in[M,B\chi]}\!\!\!\mathfrak{a}_{g}\,|M,g|_{\mathcal{C}}\in\Bbbk

is a topological invariant of
M
.

Examples of such families of coefficients can for instance be derived from the mapping space
\mathrm{TOP}(M,B\chi)
of continuous maps from
M
to
B\chi
(endowed with the compact-open topology). In particular, assume that the crossed module
\chi\colon E\to H
is finite (meaning that both
E
and
H
are finite), so that the set
[M,B\chi]=\pi_{0}(\mathrm{TOP}(M,B\chi))
and the homotopy groups of
\mathrm{TOP}(M,B\chi)
are finite. Then, assuming also that
\Bbbk
is a field of characteristic zero, we can define the family of coefficients
\mathfrak{y}=(\mathfrak{y}_{g})_{g\in[M,B\chi]}
by setting

\mathfrak{y}_{g}=\dfrac{\mathrm{card}\bigl(\pi_{2}(\mathrm{TOP}(M,B\chi),g)\bigr)}{\mathrm{card}\bigl(\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}(M,B\chi),g)\bigr)}\in\Bbbk.

For the spherical
\chi
-fusion category
\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}
of Example
4.12
, we obtain

I_{\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi},\mathfrak{y}}(M)=\mathrm{Yetter}_{\chi}(M),

where
\mathrm{Yetter}_{\chi}(M)
is the Yetter invariant of
M
associated with the finite crossed module
\chi
(see
[
Ye
]
). This follows from (
28
) and the interpretation of the Yetter invariant given in
[
FP
, Theorem 2.25]
.
More generally, for the spherical
\chi
-fusion category
\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}^{\omega}
obtained by twisting the
\chi
-category
\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}
with a normalized 3-cocycle
\omega
for
\chi
with values in
\Bbbk^{*}
(see Section
4.16
) and provided Conjecture (
29
) is true, we obtain

I_{\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}^{\omega},\mathfrak{y}}(M)=\mathrm{Yetter}_{\chi,\omega}(M),

where
\mathrm{Yetter}_{\chi,\omega}(M)
is the
\omega
-twisted Yetter invariant of
M
defined by Faria Martins and Porter in
[
FP
]
.

7.6.
Proof of Theorem
7.1

The state sum
|M|_{\mathcal{C}}
does not depend on the choice of the
\chi
-representative set
I
by the naturality of

\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}
and of the contraction vectors.
Denote the right hand side of (
27
) by
|P|_{\mathcal{C}}
. Any
\chi
-homeomorphism of
\chi
-manifolds
M\to M^{\prime}
carries a
\chi
-skeleton
P
of
M
to a
\chi
-skeleton
P^{\prime}
of
M^{\prime}
and, clearly,
|P|_{\mathcal{C}}=|P^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{C}}
. Thus, by Lemma
6.4
, we need only to prove the invariance of
|P|_{\mathcal{C}}
under the primary
\chi
-moves
P\mapsto T_{s}(P)
for all
s\in\{0,1,2,3\}
. This follows from the “local invariance” which says that the contribution of any coloring
c
of
P
to the state sum is equal to the sum of the contributions of all colorings of
T_{s}(P)
which are equal to
c
on all big regions.

Let us verify the local invariance for the bubble
\chi
-move
T_{0}
. Denote by
x
the created vertex, by

r
the region of
P
where the bubble is attached, by
D_{\pm}
the disks created by the move:

\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\raisebox{-0.35pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-0.eps}}}.

Let
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in H
be the
H
-labels and
i,k,\ell\in I
be the colors of
r,D_{+},D_{-}
, respectively. Let
e\in E
be the
E
-label of the created edge endowed with the depicted orientation and with respect to the (small)
P
-ball bounded by
D_{+}\cup D_{-}
.
Condition (
20
) becomes

\chi(e)=\beta^{\nu}\alpha^{\varepsilon}\gamma^{\mu},

where
\varepsilon,\nu,\mu
are signs depending on the orientations of the regions
r,D_{+},D_{-}
, respectively. The
\mathcal{C}
-colored
\chi
-graph in
S^{2}
associated with
x
is

\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\Gamma=\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-T0-col.eps}}}\,\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}\cup\{\infty\}=S^{2},

where the edge colored by
k
(respectively,
i,\ell
) is oriented towards the left vertex if the sign
\nu
(respectively,
\varepsilon,\mu
) is positive and towards the right vertex otherwise.
The contraction vector associated to the edge created by the move is the contraction vector
\ast
determined by the duality between the two vertices of
\Gamma
induced by the symmetry with respect to a vertical great circle. Now, using (
19
) and (
12
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Gamma)(\ast)=\;\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{tensor-contract-col-1.eps}}}\;=\;\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{tensor-contract-col-2.eps}}}\,\;=N^{\mathbb{1},e}_{k^{\nu}\otimes i^{\varepsilon}\otimes\ell^{\mu}}.

Then the contribution of
x
to the state sum is

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in I_{\beta}\\
\ell\in I_{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\dim(k)\dim(\ell)\,\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Gamma)(\ast)\overset{(i)}{=}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in I_{\beta}\\
\ell\in I_{\gamma}\end{subarray}}\dim(k)\dim(\ell)\,N^{\mathbb{1},e}_{k^{\nu}\otimes i^{\varepsilon}\otimes\ell^{\mu}}

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m\in I_{\beta^{\nu}}\\
n\in I_{\gamma^{\mu}}\end{subarray}}\dim(m)\dim(n)\,N^{\mathbb{1},e}_{m\otimes i^{\varepsilon}\otimes n}\overset{(iii)}{=}\dim(i^{\varepsilon})\dim(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})\overset{(iv)}{=}\dim(i)\dim(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1}).

Here
(i)
follows from the previous computation,

(ii)
and
(iv)
from the equality
\dim(X^{*})=\dim(X)
for any
X\in\mathcal{C}
, and
(iii)
from Lemma
4.1
applied with
X=\mathbb{1}
and
Y=i^{\varepsilon}
. The factor
\dim(i)\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})
is compensated by the change in the Euler characteristic of the big region and in
the number of components of
M\setminus P
.

Let us verify the local invariance for the phantom edge
\chi
-move
T_{1}
.
This move adds a new edge
e
connecting two distinct vertices of
P
. This modifies the link graphs of these vertices by adding a new vertex
u
(respectively,
v
) inside an edge. The
E
-label of
e
is
1\in E
and so, by (
20
), the (possibly coinciding) regions
r
and
r^{\prime}
of
T_{1}(P)
lying on the two sides of
e
have the same
H
-label
h\in H
. The colorings of
T_{1}(P)
assigning different colors to the
regions
r
and
r^{\prime}
contribute zero to the state sum (because
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(i,j)=0
for distinct
i,j\in I
). The colorings of
T_{1}(P)
assigning the same color
i\in I
to the
regions
r
and
r^{\prime}
contribute the same as the colorings of
P
assigning
i
to the region of
P

containing
e
. Indeed,

\displaystyle\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\!\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{inv-Xi-graph-prefusion-cup1a.eps}}}\,\right)\otimes\,\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\!\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{inv-Xi-graph-prefusion-cup1b.eps}}}\,\right)\,(*_{e})

\displaystyle=\dim(i)^{-1}\;\psfrag\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\!\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{inv-Xi-graph-prefusion-cup2.eps}}}\,\right)\otimes\,\psfrag\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\!\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{inv-Xi-graph-prefusion-cup3.eps}}}\,\right)

and the factor
\dim(i)^{-1}
is compensated by the change in the Euler characteristics of the regions.
The latter equality follows from the fact that
\ast_{e}
is induced by the inverse
\Omega
of the pairing

\omega\colon H_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}H_{i}\to\Bbbk,\quad u\otimes_{\Bbbk}v\mapsto\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{omega-S-col.eps}}}

where
H_{i}=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(\mathbb{1},i^{*}\otimes i)
, and that
\Omega\colon\Bbbk\to H_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}H_{i}
is computed by

\Omega(1_{\Bbbk})=\dim(i)^{-1}\,\widetilde{\mathrm{coev}}_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\widetilde{\mathrm{coev}}_{i}

because
H_{i}\simeq\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(i)
is free
\Bbbk
-module of rank 1 and

\omega(\widetilde{\mathrm{coev}}_{i}\otimes_{\Bbbk}\widetilde{\mathrm{coev}}_{i})=\,\psfrag\raisebox{-0.4pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{omega-coev-i-col.eps}}}\,=\,\psfrag\raisebox{-0.4pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{omega-coev-i-2-col.eps}}}\!=\dim(i).

Let us verify the local invariance for the contraction
\chi
-move
T_{2}
.
This move collapses an edge of
P
between distinct vertices
x,x^{\prime}
of
P
into a vertex
y
of
T_{2}(P)
. We can assume that
x
is an endpoint of some other edge. Let
e\in E
be the
E
-label of the collapsed edge oriented from
x
to
x^{\prime}
and with respect to the
P
-ball branch represented by the upper half-space. Note that the colorings of
P
and
T_{2}(P)
are the same. Given such a coloring, the
\mathcal{C}
-colored
\chi
-graphs
\Gamma_{x},\Gamma_{x^{\prime}}
in
S^{2}
associated with
x,x^{\prime}
are of the following form:

\psfrag\psfrag\Gamma_{x}=\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3p.eps}}}\quad\text{and}\quad\Gamma_{x^{\prime}}=\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3q.eps}}}

where white boxes stand for a piece of a
\mathcal{C}
-colored
\chi
-graph and the depicted vertices are in duality (induced by the collapsed edge). Denote by
\ast
the contraction vector determined by this duality.
The
\mathcal{C}
-colored
\chi
-graph
\Gamma_{y}
in
S^{2}
associated with
y
is obtained from
\Gamma_{x}
and
\Gamma_{x^{\prime}}
by joining their dual vertices:

\psfrag\psfrag\Gamma_{y}=\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3v.eps}}}\,\;.

Then the contribution of
x,x^{\prime}
to the state sum
|P|_{\mathcal{C}}
is

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Gamma_{x})\otimes\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Gamma_{x^{\prime}})\right)(\ast)\overset{(i)}{=}\psfrag\psfrag\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3r.eps}}}\,\right)(\ast)

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{=}\sum_{i\in I_{1}}\dim(i)\;\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3t.eps}}}\,\right)(\ast)\overset{(iii)}{=}\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}(\Gamma_{y})

and thus is equal to the contribution of
y
to the state sum
|T_{3}(P)|_{\mathcal{C}}
.
Here
(i)
follows from the
\otimes
-multiplicativity of
\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}
(see Section
5.6
),

(ii)
from the facts that the
\chi
-graph
\Gamma_{x}
is 1-spherical,
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{C}}^{1}(i,\mathbb{1})=0

for all
i\in I_{1}
distinct from
\mathbb{1}
, and
\dim(\mathbb{1})=1_{\Bbbk}
, and
(iii)
from Lemma
5.2
since the element
h
in that lemma is equal to
\chi(e)
by (
20
).

Finally, let us verify the local invariance for the percolation
\chi
-move
T_{3}
.
This move pushes a branch
b
of
P
through a vertex
x
of
P
and across a small disk
D
lying in another branch
b^{\prime}
of
P
at
x
. Denote by
y
the vertex of
T_{3}(P)
corresponding to
x
:

\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\raisebox{-0.35pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3n.eps}}}.

Let
\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in H
be the
H
-labels and
m,k,\ell\in I
be the colors of
D,b,b^{\prime}
, respectively. Let
e\in E
be the
E
-label of
\partial D
endowed with the depicted orientation and with respect to the
P
-ball branch represented by the upper half-space above
D
. Condition (
20
) becomes

\chi(e)=\alpha^{-\varepsilon}\gamma^{\mu}\beta^{\nu},

where
\varepsilon,\nu,\mu
are signs depending on the orientations of the regions
D,b,b^{\prime}
, respectively. The
\mathcal{C}
-colored
\chi
-graph in
S^{2}
associated with
y
is obtained from
\mathcal{C}
-colored
\chi
-graph in
S^{2}
associated with
x
by locally replacing

with

where the edge colored by
k
(respectively,
\ell,m
) is oriented downwards if the sign
\nu
(respectively,
\mu,\varepsilon
) is positive and upwards otherwise.
The contraction vector associated to the edge
\partial D
is the contraction vector
\ast
determined by the duality between the two vertices (in the above right picture) induced by the symmetry with respect to a horizontal great circle.
Then the contribution of
y
to the state sum
|T_{3}(P)|_{\mathcal{C}}
is

\displaystyle\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\sum_{m\in I_{\alpha}}\dim(m)\;\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3e.eps}}}\,\right)(\ast)

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{=}\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\psfrag\sum_{i\in I_{\alpha^{\varepsilon}}}\dim(i)\;\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3c.eps}}}\,\right)(\ast)\;\overset{(ii)}{=}\;\psfrag\psfrag\mathbb{F}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\,\raisebox{-0.45pt}{\scalebox{.9}{\includegraphics[]{proof-Xi-M-inv-3d.eps}}}\,\right)

and thus is equal to the contribution of
x
to the state sum
|P|_{\mathcal{C}}
.
Here
(i)
follows from the equality
\dim(X^{*})=\dim(X)
for any
X\in\mathcal{C}
and
(ii)
from Lemma
5.2
since
\alpha^{\varepsilon}=\gamma^{\mu}\beta^{\nu}\chi(e^{-1})
.

7.7.
Remark

The right-hand side of Formula (
27
) is the product of
\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})^{-|M\setminus P|}
and a certain sum which we denote

\Sigma_{\mathcal{C}}(P)
. The definition of
\Sigma_{\mathcal{C}}(P)\in\Bbbk
does not use the assumption that
\dim(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})
is invertible in
\Bbbk
and applies to an arbitrary spherical
\chi
-fusion category
\mathcal{C}
. This allows us to generalize the invariant
|M,g|_{\mathcal{C}}
of a closed
\chi
-manifold
(M,g)
to any such
\mathcal{C}
. We use the theory of spines, see
[
Mat
]
. By a spine of
M
, we mean an oriented stratified 2-polyhedron
P\subset M
such that
P
has at least 2 vertices,

P
is locally homeomorphic to the cone over the 1-skeleton of a tetrahedron, and
M\setminus P
is an open ball. By
[
Mat
]
,
M
has a spine
P
and any two spines of
M
can be related by the moves
T^{1,2}
,
T^{2,1}
in the class of spines. These moves lift to
\chi
-moves and the arguments of Section
7.6
imply that
\Sigma_{\mathcal{C}}(P)
is preserved under the
\chi
-moves
T^{1,2}
,
T^{2,1}
. Therefore
||M,g||_{\mathcal{C}}=\Sigma_{\mathcal{C}}(P)
is a topological invariant of
(M,g)
. If
\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})
is invertible, then
||M,g||_{\mathcal{C}}=\dim(\mathcal{C}_{1}^{1})\,|M,g|_{\mathcal{C}}
.

7.8.
The case of pointed
\chi
-manifolds

For pointed topological spaces
X,Y
, we denote by
[X,Y]_{*}
the set of pointed homotopy classes of pointed maps
X\to Y
.
Note that if
X
is a connected CW-complex with basepoint a 0-cell and if
Y
is path-connected, then
\pi_{1}(Y)
acts (on the right) on
[X,Y]_{*}
and the canonical map
[X,Y]_{*}\to[X,Y]
induces a bijection

[X,Y]_{*}/\pi_{1}(Y)\simeq[X,Y].

By a
pointed closed
\chi
-manifold
, we mean a pair
(M,g)
where
M
is a pointed closed oriented 3-dimensional manifold and
g\in[M,B\chi]_{*}
. Here
B\chi
is pointed by its unique 0-cell (see Section
2.4
).
Then the invariant
|\cdot|_{\mathcal{C}}
of closed
\chi
-manifolds from Theorem
7.1
extends trivially to pointed closed
\chi
-manifolds by setting

|M,g|_{\mathcal{C}}=|M,\upsilon(g)|_{\mathcal{C}}

for any pointed closed
\chi
-manifold
(M,g)
, where
\upsilon(g)
is the image of
g
under the canonical map
[M,B\chi]_{*}\to[M,B\chi]
.

7.9.
The case of push-forwards

Let
\phi=(\psi\colon E\to E^{\prime},\varphi\colon H\to H^{\prime})
be a morphism from a crossed module
\chi\colon E\to H
to a crossed module
\chi^{\prime}\colon E^{\prime}\to H^{\prime}
such that
\psi
and
\varphi
are surjective,
\mathrm{Ker}(\psi)\cap\mathrm{Ker}(\chi)=1
, and
\mathrm{Ker}(\varphi)
is finite. Let
\mathcal{C}
be a spherical
\chi
-fusion category. By Section
4.14
, the push-forward
\phi_{*}(\mathcal{C})
of
\mathcal{C}
is a
\chi^{\prime}
-fusion category such that

\dim\bigr(\phi_{*}(\mathcal{C})^{1}_{1}\bigl)=d_{\phi}\dim(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})\quad\text{with}\quad d_{\phi}=\frac{\mathrm{card}\bigr(\mathrm{Ker}(\varphi)\bigl)}{\mathrm{card}\bigr(\mathrm{Ker}(\psi)\bigl)}\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

Assume that
\mathrm{Ker}(\chi^{\prime})
is finite and that
\mathrm{card}\bigr(\mathrm{Ker}(\varphi)\bigl)
,
\mathrm{card}\bigr(\mathrm{Ker}(\chi^{\prime})\bigl)
, and
\dim(\mathcal{C}^{1}_{1})
are invertible in
\Bbbk
. Then
d_{\phi}
and
\dim(\phi_{*}(\mathcal{C})^{1}_{1})
are also invertible. Moreover, since
\psi
induces an injective group homomorphism
\mathrm{Ker}(\chi)\to\mathrm{Ker}(\chi^{\prime})
(because
\chi^{\prime}\psi=\varphi\chi
and
\mathrm{Ker}(\psi)\cap\mathrm{Ker}(\chi)=1
), we get that
\mathrm{card}\bigr(\mathrm{Ker}(\chi)\bigl)
divides
\mathrm{card}\bigr(\mathrm{Ker}(\chi^{\prime})\bigl)
and so is finite and invertible in
\Bbbk
.

By Section
7.8
, the category
\phi_{*}(\mathcal{C})
defines the invariant
|\cdot|_{\phi_{*}(\mathcal{C})}
of pointed closed
\chi^{\prime}
-manifolds and the category
\mathcal{C}
defines the invariant
|\cdot|_{\mathcal{C}}
of pointed closed
\chi
-manifolds. The next theorem computes the former from the latter. A (pointed) closed
\chi
-manifold is
connected
if the underlying manifold is connected.

Theorem 7.2
.

Let
(M,g^{\prime})
be a connected pointed closed
\chi^{\prime}
-manifold.
Then

|M,g^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\mathcal{C})}=d_{\phi}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in[M,B\chi]_{*}\\
B\phi\circ g=g^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\dfrac{\mathrm{card}\bigl(\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}_{*}(M,B\chi^{\prime}),g^{\prime})\bigr)}{\mathrm{card}\bigl(\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}_{*}(M,B\chi),g)\bigr)}\,|M,g|_{\mathcal{C}},

where
\mathrm{TOP}_{*}(X,Y)
denotes the space of pointed continuous maps from
X
to
Y
(endowed with the compact-open topology) and
B\phi\colon B\chi\to B\chi^{\prime}
is the (pointed) map induced by
\phi
.

Note that the finiteness of the above sum follows from the fact that
\mathrm{Ker}(\varphi)
is finite. Also, the involved fundamental groups are finite with invertible cardinal in
\Bbbk
(since
M
is compact and
\mathrm{Ker}(\chi)=\pi_{2}(B\chi)
and
\mathrm{Ker}(\chi^{\prime})=\pi_{2}(B\chi^{\prime})
are finite with invertible cardinals, see Claim
7.16
below). We prove Theorem
7.2
in Section
7.12
.

In the case of groups, that is, when
E
and
E^{\prime}
are trivial, the fundamental groups

\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}(M,B\chi),g)
and
\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}(M,B\chi^{\prime}),g^{\prime})
are trivial,
d_{\phi}=\mathrm{card}\bigr(\mathrm{Ker}(\varphi)\bigl)
, and Theorem
7.2
gives back the relationship given in the appendix of
[
TVi1
]
.

If
\phi
is an equivalence of crossed modules (meaning that
\psi
and
\varphi
respectively induce isomorphisms
\mathrm{Ker}(\chi)\cong\mathrm{Ker}(\chi^{\prime})
and
\mathrm{Coker}(\chi)\cong\mathrm{Coker}(\chi^{\prime})
), then
B\phi
is a homotopy equivalence (by the Whitehead theorem),
d_{\phi}=1
, and Theorem
7.2
gives

|M,g^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\mathcal{C})}=|M,(B\phi)^{-1}\circ g^{\prime}|_{\mathcal{C}}

where
(B\phi)^{-1}
is a homotopy inverse of
B\phi
.

7.10.
Example

Consider the spherical
\chi
-fusion category
\mathcal{C}=\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi}
of Example
4.12
.
Then Theorem
7.2
together with (
28
) implies that for any connected pointed closed
\chi^{\prime}
-manifold
(M,g^{\prime})
,

(30)

|M,g^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi})}=d_{\phi}^{-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in[M,B\chi]_{*}\\
B\phi\circ g=g^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\dfrac{\mathrm{card}\bigl(\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}_{*}(M,B\chi^{\prime}),g^{\prime})\bigr)}{\mathrm{card}\bigl(\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}_{*}(M,B\chi),g)\bigr)}1_{\Bbbk}.

Let us illustrate this with the following explicit morphism of crossed modules: consider finite abelian groups
A,G
with invertible cardinal in
\Bbbk
and such that
H^{2}(G,A)=0
. Let

\phi=\left(\vbox{\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\kern 11.2917pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.21016pt\raise 5.1875pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\chi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 26.66684pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-11.2917pt\raise-12.70842pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.75pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\psi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-19.91684pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 26.66684pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\times G\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.45915pt\raise-13.31952pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-0.8264pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\varphi}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 43.45915pt\raise-20.30571pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise-27.25015pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.50388pt\raise-33.82849pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.21725pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\chi^{\prime}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 37.95915pt\raise-27.25015pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 37.95915pt\raise-27.25015pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{1}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}}\right)

where
\chi(a)=(a,1)
for all
a\in A
, the action of
A\times G
on
A
is trivial, and
\psi=\mathrm{id}_{A}
.
Note that
\mathrm{Ker}(\chi)=1
and so
\mathrm{Ker}(\psi)\cap\mathrm{Ker}(\chi)=1
. Also
\mathrm{Ker}(\varphi)=A\times G
and
\mathrm{Ker}(\chi^{\prime})=A
are finite with invertible cardinal in
\Bbbk
. Using (i) of Section
2.4
, we obtain that the classifying spaces
B\chi
and
B\chi^{\prime}
are Eilenberg-MacLane spaces of type
K(G,1)
and
K(A,2)
, respectively. Recall that
[X,K(B,n)]_{*}\simeq H^{n}(X,B)
for any CW-complex
X
, abelian group
B
, and
n\geq 1
.
Then the induced map
B\phi\colon B\chi\to B\chi^{\prime}
is (pointed) null-homotopic because

[B\chi,B\chi^{\prime}]_{*}\simeq H^{2}(B\chi,A)\simeq H^{2}(G,A)=0.

Let
M
be a connected pointed closed oriented 3-dimensional manifold. For any
g^{\prime}\in[X,B\chi^{\prime}]_{*}
which is not null-homotopic, there are no
g\in[M,B\chi]_{*}
such that
B\phi\circ g=g^{\prime}
(since
B\phi
is null-homotopic) and so Formula (
30
) gives that

|M,g^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi})}=0.

Let
c^{\prime}\in[M,B\chi^{\prime}]_{*}
be the null-homotopy class. For any
g\in[M,B\chi]_{*}
, we have
B\phi\circ g=c^{\prime}
(because
B\phi
is null-homotopic) and
\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}_{*}(M,B\chi),g)=1
(because
B\chi
is a
K(G,1)
space). Since
d_{\phi}=\mathrm{card}(A)\mathrm{card}(G)
,
[M,B\chi]_{*}\simeq H^{1}(M,G)
, and

\displaystyle\pi_{1}(\mathrm{TOP}_{*}(M,B\chi^{\prime}),c^{\prime})\simeq[S^{1},(\mathrm{TOP}_{*}(M,B\chi^{\prime}),c^{\prime})]_{*}

\displaystyle\simeq[M\wedge S^{1},B\chi^{\prime}]_{*}\simeq H^{2}(M\wedge S^{1},A)\simeq H^{1}(M,A),

Formula (
30
) gives that

|M,c^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\Bbbk\mathcal{G}_{\chi})}=\dfrac{\mathrm{card}\bigl(H^{1}(M,A))\bigr)}{\mathrm{card}(A)}\,\dfrac{\mathrm{card}\bigl(H^{1}(M,G)\bigr)}{\mathrm{card}(G)}1_{\Bbbk}.

For example, assume that
\Bbbk=\mathbb{Q}
,
A=\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
,
G=\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z}
, and
M=\mathbb{R}P^{3}
. (Note that we do have
H^{2}(G,A)=H^{2}(\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z},\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})=0
.) Since

[\mathbb{R}P^{3},B\chi^{\prime}]_{*}=H^{2}(\mathbb{R}P^{3},\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},

there are two distinct pointed homotopy classes from
\mathbb{R}P^{3}
to
B\chi^{\prime}
, namely the null-homotopy class
c^{\prime}
and another class
g^{\prime}\neq c^{\prime}
. Using that
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}P^{3},\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z})\simeq\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
and
H^{1}(\mathbb{R}P^{3},\mathbb{Z}/3\mathbb{Z})=0
, the above computations give:

|\mathbb{R}P^{3},c^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\mathbb{Q}\mathcal{G}_{\chi})}=\frac{1}{3}\quad\text{and}\quad|\mathbb{R}P^{3},g^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\mathbb{Q}\mathcal{G}_{\chi})}=0.

In particular,
|\mathbb{R}P^{3},c^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\mathbb{Q}\mathcal{G}_{\chi})}\neq|\mathbb{R}P^{3},g^{\prime}|_{\phi_{*}(\mathbb{Q}\mathcal{G}_{\chi})}
. Since
\pi_{1}(B\chi^{\prime})=1
, we have:

[\mathbb{R}P^{3},B\chi^{\prime}]\simeq[\mathbb{R}P^{3},B\chi^{\prime}]_{*}.

Also, all the maps
\mathbb{R}P^{3}\to B\chi^{\prime}
are phantom maps (meaning that they induce trivial homomorphisms on homotopy groups) because
\pi_{2}(\mathbb{R}P^{3})=1
and
B\chi^{\prime}
is a
K(\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z},2)
space. This example shows that the invariant
|\cdot|_{\mathcal{C}}
of Theorem
7.1
is nontrivial and may distinguish distinct homotopy classes of phantom maps.

7.11.
Example

Let
\chi\colon E\to H
be a crossed module such that
E
is finite and assume that
\Bbbk
is a field of characteristic zero. Then the category
\chi\mbox{-}\,\mathrm{vect}_{\Bbbk}
of Example
4.15
is a spherical

-fusion category with
\dim\bigr((\chi\mbox{-}\,\mathrm{vect}_{\Bbbk})^{1}_{1}\bigl)=\mathrm{card}(E)1_{\Bbbk}\neq 0
. Recall from Section
2.4
the canonical inclusion
BH\hookrightarrow B\chi
. Then, for any connected pointed closed
\chi
-manifold
(M,g)
, we have:

(31)

|M,g|_{\chi\mbox{-}\,\mathrm{vect}_{\Bbbk}}\neq 0\;\Leftrightarrow\;\text{$g$ factorizes through $BH\hookrightarrow B\chi$.}

Indeed, recall that
\chi\mbox{-}\,\mathrm{vect}_{\Bbbk}=\phi_{*}((E,H\ltimes E)\,\mbox{-}\,\mathrm{vect}_{\Bbbk})
